AI 資金壓力拖累科技股，惟產業需求仍具韌性
2026 年 10 月 23 日
一、 市場動態
1. 費半再度重挫，科技股成為美股主要賣壓來源：美國股市主要指數 10 月 22 日跌多漲
少， 道瓊工業指數上漲0.02%，收52,867.98 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.21%，收7,663.43
點；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 2.60%，收 26,008.86 點，創 6 月 5 日以來最大單日跌幅
。費城半導體指數重挫 6.72%，收 10,731.98 點，跌幅明顯大於主要股價指數，顯示
AI 及半導體族群仍是本波市場修正的主要壓力來源。

2. AI 相關企業全面承壓 ， 資金流向非科技股及公債 ：10 月 22 日 Meta 下跌約 7%、Lam
Research 跌幅逾一成，Nvidia 下跌 4.4%，其他 AI 相關股票亦明顯走低，顯示市場
對 AI 資本支出及投資報酬的疑慮已由大型科技平台延伸至半導體與基礎建設供應商
。不過，道瓊上漲 0.02%，羅素 2000 指數亦小幅收紅，反映資金並未全面撤出股市
；美國 10 年期公債殖利率則因避險需求跌破 5%，顯示資金由高評價科技股轉向其他
產業及避險資產。
3. 中東地緣政治風險升溫，油價攀至 5 月以來高點：中東能源運輸及供給風險 10 月 22
日再度升高，布蘭特原油價格上漲約 3.76%，升至每桶 113 美元附近，創 5 月以來高
點。油價攀升除使市場重新關注能源成本對通膨與 Fed 政策路徑的影響，也可能增加
AI 資料中心的電力及營運成本。中東情勢雖非本次半導體股重挫的唯一因素，但在市
場已擔憂 AI 投資效益的情況下，能源價格上揚進一步壓抑投資人風險偏好。

二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 殖利率回落難抵科技股下跌，顯示修正因素出現轉變：10 月 14 日費半重挫主要受到
美國 9 月 CPI 高於預期、升息疑慮及美債殖利率盤中急升影響，惟當日 10 年期殖利
率收盤反而小幅下降；10 月 22 日情況則更加明顯，美國 10 年期公債殖利率因避險
需求盤中跌破 5%，費半仍大跌 6.72%。這顯示市場關注焦點已由單純的利率與估值
壓力，逐步轉向 AI 資本支出的資金來源、融資能力及投資回收期。因此，即使後續殖
利率回落，若企業仍無法證明AI 投資能轉化為足夠營收與現金流，科技股評價仍可能
持續受到壓抑。
2. Meta 自由現金流轉負 ，AI 投資成本由預期轉為實際財務壓力 ：Meta 第三季營收 646.0
億美元、年增 26%，顯示核心業務仍維持強勁成長；但資本支出達 349.1 億美元，超
過同期營業現金流 327.9 億美元，使自由現金流由去年同期正 106.3 億美元轉為負
21.2 億美元。公司亦將全年資本支出預估調整至 1,350～1,450 億美元，並宣布設立

無表決權 Class C 股票，增加未來資本運用彈性。這反映 AI 投資規模已開始超越部分

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
大型科技企業當期現金創造能力，但 Meta 仍持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有
價證券，不能將單季自由現金流轉負直接解讀為財務危機。
3. 資本稀缺逐漸成為 AI 擴張瓶頸，新興雲端業者面臨更大壓力：AI 發展需要持續投入
GPU、伺服器、資料中心及電力基礎建設，龐大資金需求使融資能力逐漸成為決定企
業成長速度的重要因素。相較具備穩定營業現金流與龐大資產規模的大型科技平台，
新興 AI 雲端服務商如 CoreWeave 及 Nebius 高度依賴外部融資，因而更容易受到借
貸成本、信用利差及投資人風險偏好變化影響。我們認為，這次修正並不代表 AI 運算

需求突然消失，而是市場開始重新評估不同企業能否負擔下一階段的擴張成本。
4. 半導體設備財報強勁卻遭賣壓，市場提高獲利兌現標準：Lam Research 於 10 月 21
日公布截至 2026 年 9 月季度（FY2027 Q1）營收 82.19 億美元，年增 54.4%、季增
22%，非 GAAP 營業利益率達 40.2%，下一季營收指引為 86 億美元、上下浮動 4 億
美元。然而，即使營運仍快速成長，10 月 22 日股價仍大幅下跌。我們研判，市場已
由過去重視 AI 訂單與營收成長，轉向同時檢視未來成長能否超越原先偏高預期，以及
AI 投資是否具備足夠的財務報酬。當股價已反映高度成長期待，即使財報表現良好，
也可能因評價調整與籌碼降溫而承壓。
5. 油價再度上漲，Fed 政策風險仍可能限制科技股評價：儘管 10 月 22 日美債殖利率因
避險需求下滑，但能源價格上漲仍可能延緩通膨降溫，使 Fed 後續政策路徑維持不確
定性。若油價持續高檔，不僅可能推升企業運輸、電力及營運成本，也將增加通膨預

期，進而限制長天期美債殖利率的下行空間。因此，科技股短線同時面臨AI 投資報酬
受到重新檢視 ， 以及能源價格與利率政策不確定的雙重壓力 。 後續10 月 28 日 FOMC
會後聲明及主席記者會，將是評估利率環境的重要依據。
三、 市場看法：
1. 股市下跌但多數個股上漲 ， 顯示賣壓集中於大型科技股 ：10 月 22 日 S&P 500 指數下
跌 1.21%，但根據美聯社（AP）報導，約三分之二成分股逆勢上漲，追蹤 S&P 500
等權重指數的 ETF（RSP）更上漲 0.73%，道瓊工業指數亦小幅收紅，呈現市值加權
指數與市場廣度明顯背離的現象 。這反映本波修正主要集中於權重較高的 AI 及大型科
技股，而非整體美股同步走弱。相較上週以防禦性類股輪動為主的情況，本週更值得
注意的是市場對高資本支出及高評價科技企業的重新定價，資金仍持續流向其他產業
，而非全面性風險撤離。只要賣壓未進一步擴散，現階段仍較接近科技股評價調整與
類股輪動，而非整體美股多頭格局反轉。
2. 台積電財測再度上修，顯示 AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉：台積電 10 月 15 日法說會

將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先略高於 40%上修至約 44%， 第三季營收達新台幣
1.49 兆元、年增 50.9%，稅後純益年增 68.7%，每股盈餘 29.43 元、同樣年增 68.7%
；第四季美元營收預估為 523～543 億美元，以中間值計算季增約 14%。此外，Lam
Research 截至 9 月季度營收年增 54.4%，亦顯示半導體設備需求仍強勁。前述這些

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元

不另行通知。
數據與近期科技股重挫形成明顯對比 ， 反映目前市場主要擔憂AI 投資回收及股票評價
，而非半導體終端需求同步惡化。只要大型雲端服務商未全面縮減AI 資本支出，先進
製程與設備需求中期仍具支撐。
3. AI 投資進入報酬驗證階段，營收成長能否追上資本投入成為關鍵：我們認為，AI 產業
目前正由過去的資本支出擴張階段，逐步轉向投資報酬率與自由現金流的驗證階段。
以 Meta 為例，第三季營收仍成長 26%，但資本支出已超過營業現金流，顯示即使
AI 技術有助於改善廣告推薦及營收變現，龐大基礎建設投入仍可能使短期自由現金流

承壓。不過，資料中心及運算設備通常具有多年使用年限，不能僅以單季現金流評估
整體投資成敗。真正關鍵在於未來 AI 服務收入、營業利益及現金流成長，能否逐步抵
銷前期資本投入。具備明確商業化模式、較高資本效率及穩定現金流的企業，後續表
現可望優於高度依賴融資與成長預期的業者。
4. AI 融資風險仍集中於部分企業 ， 尚不足以認定信用危機全面擴散 ：Meta Q3 財報顯示
，該公司持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有價證券，顯示其具備一定財務緩衝能
力；相較之下，新興AI 雲端業者因資料中心建置成本龐大、現金流尚未成熟，對信用
市場與外部融資條件的敏感度相對較高 。雖然 AI 資本支出引發投資人重新評估企業信
用風險，但我們認為目前仍缺乏足夠證據認定風險已擴散至整體投資級債市場或銀行
體系。然而，後續仍宜密切觀察 AI 相關企業 CDS 與公司債利差變化、再融資能力及
主要金融機構授信條件對於科技類股及整體股債市場的後續影響。
5. 股價修正不代表企業獲利同步下修，財報表現仍有機會支撐科技股：儘管近期半導體

及 AI 相關股價回落，但台積電與Lam Research 最新財報顯示，部分供應鏈企業營收
及獲利仍維持高度成長，反映股票價格與企業基本面短期可能出現明顯落差。當市場
因融資及資本支出疑慮而降低評價倍數時，即使企業獲利持續增加，股價也可能暫時
承壓。然而，若後續大型科技公司公布的雲端營收、AI 訂單與獲利展望持續優於預期
，企業 EPS 上修仍有機會抵銷部分本益比壓縮壓力。因此，目前尚不宜將科技股價格
修正直接等同於 AI 產業景氣反轉，企業獲利能否持續兌現，仍是中期市場方向的重要
支撐。

大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
6. Fed 與大型科技股財報進入驗證期，短線震盪可能延續：Fed 將於 10 月 27～28 日舉
行利率決策會議，市場除關注是否調整政策利率外，更應重視會後聲明及主席記者會
對通膨、油價與後續利率路徑的態度。另一方面，大型雲端服務商後續公布的財報，
將提供 AI 資本支出、雲端營收及投資效率的最新證據。短線科技股仍可能受到市場評
價偏高、融資風險及地緣政治因素影響而維持震盪；不過，只要企業獲利成長與 AI
需求未出現結構性反轉，中期科技產業基本面仍具支撐，現階段維持短線審慎、中期
偏正向的看法。
四、市場展望：

1. 科技股短線宜審慎觀察 ， 中期仍看好具基本面支撐的AI 產業 ：近期科技股受到 AI 資本支
出、融資能力與評價調整等因素影響，短線波動可能延續，尤其高度依賴外部資金及獲利
尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者，仍面臨較大不確定性。然而，台積電及 Lam Research 最
新財報顯示，先進製程與半導體設備需求尚未出現全面轉弱。我們認為，科技股中期展望
仍偏正向，但市場將更重視企業營收成長、訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率。相較缺
乏穩定獲利的 AI 概念企業，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健及獲利成長能見度的科技企業，
風險報酬相對有利。
2. AI 產業由全面成長轉向基本面分化，現金流將成為選股關鍵：隨著大型科技企業持續擴
大 AI 基礎建設投資，市場對未來投資報酬率的要求將同步提高。就目前已公布的科技股
財報結果來看，AI 產業長期成長趨勢尚未改變，但後續股價表現將更加分化，具備穩定
現金流、明確訂單及商業化能力的企業，相對有利；反之，對於高度依賴外部融資、自由
現金流持續為負且缺乏明確獲利時程的企業，仍宜維持審慎。

3. 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：半導體類股 10/22 重挫，美國公債
價格上揚、10 年期殖利率盤中跌破 5%，顯示股市風險偏好下降時，美債仍可提供一定
避險效果。雖然 Fed 政策路徑及能源價格仍存在不確定性，但預期相較受政策利率影響
較大的短天期公債，以及受財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價影響的超長天期公債，中天期
美國公債的風險報酬仍相對較為均衡 。 投資級債因企業信用基本面整體仍具韌性 ， 中期維
持偏正向看法；高收益債則須留意 AI 相關發行人信用風險上升及利差擴大的影響。

美期中選舉牽動市場表現，股債配置重品質、控風險
2026 年 11 月 04 日
一、 市場動態
1. 美期中選舉牽動市場情緒，亞股一度隨美股期貨下挫：美國 11 月 3 日舉行期中選舉
，根據 CNN 網站的結果顯示，截至台北時間 11/4 下午 3 點左右，民主黨於參議院
已取得 100 席中的 53 席的過半席次，於眾議院則在 435 席中取得了 225 席(218 席
過半)。市場重新評估國會控制權及川普政府未來政策推動能力，擔心民主黨國會影響
力擴大 ，可能對財政政策及科技企業監管形成新的制衡 。S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100

期貨在開票期間一度下挫 1.38%及 2.35%，科技股明顯承受較大賣壓。惟隨後跌深反
彈，截至台北時間下午 3 時左右，S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100 期貨跌幅已收斂至
0.22%及 0.44%。亞洲市場方面，科技股為主及出口導向股市受到美股期貨拖累，台
灣加權指數 11/4 盤中最低下挫 3.38%，所幸隨跌幅收斂，終場下跌 0.83%；韓股同
日也由大跌近 4%由黑翻紅上漲 0.62%。11/3 未開盤的日股，在 11/4 開盤後出現補
跌，最低下挫近 4.8%，但最後跌幅收斂至下跌 1.42%。
2. 美國公債及美元同步反映政策預期調整，市場避險情緒升溫：美國公債及美元反映政
策預期調整，殖利率盤中先跌後漲：10 年期公債殖利率盤中先跌後漲，截至台北時間
下午 3 時來到 4.906%；同一時間，美元指數小跌約 0.37%。股、債、匯市的變動顯
示，投資人除了調整股票風險部位，也在評估未來美國財政刺激規模、政府債務供給
與利率前景。
二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨取得國會控制權，市場重新定價財政與監管政策：本次選舉對金融市場的主要

影響，在於未來行政與立法部門的權力分配可能改變。共和黨先前同時掌握白宮與國
會，有利於推動稅制、財政及產業政策；民主黨在參眾兩院均取得過半席次後，部分

政策將面臨更高的協商成本。財政方面，新增減稅及擴大支出的空間可能受到限制，
市場因而下修部分財政刺激預期；企業方面，國會監督權可能增加對大型科技企業、
AI 資料中心、反壟斷及資料隱私議題的調查壓力。惟國會控制權改變不代表既有政策
立即逆轉，也不代表所有監管措施均能完成立法。此次市場反應更可能是投資人重新
要求政策風險溢酬，而非企業獲利已發生實質下修。
2. AI 資本支出與投資回報疑慮尚未消退，放大科技股對政治事件的反應：此次選舉開票
期間，Nasdaq 期貨及與科技相關的亞股出現大幅波動，亦與近期科技股基本面及評

價爭議有關。10 月底以來美股因大型科技企業 AI 投資效益受到質疑而大幅修正，市
場關注焦點逐漸由 AI 基礎建設支出規模 ， 轉向相關投資能否創造足夠的營運現金流與
股東報酬。以雲端服務及資料中心產業而言，企業雖可透過AI 運算需求增加帶動營收
成長，但 GPU、伺服器、電力設備及資料中心建設需要龐大前期資金，若營收回收速
度不如預期，可能使自由現金流承壓並增加外部融資需求。近期部分科技企業籌資計
畫及財測不佳，已使投資人提高對財務品質的要求。當市場原先已對AI 投資回報存有
疑慮，新的政治及監管不確定性便更容易放大評價修正幅度。
三、 市場看法
1. 半導體需求仍具分化 ， 政治事件不等於AI 產業成長趨勢反轉 ：科技股近期的股價修正
與 AI 終端需求惡化並非相同概念。AMD 近期公布優於市場預期的財報，並對資料中
心業務提出較強勁的成長展望，顯示 AI 運算需求仍具有一定韌性；相對而言，Intel
先前財測疲弱及現金流壓力，則反映不同半導體企業的產品競爭力、資本效率及終端
市場曝險存在明顯差異。因此，不宜將半導體股整體下跌解讀為AI 需求全面降溫。從

產業鏈觀察，具備技術領先優勢、穩定客戶需求及良好現金流的企業，與高度依賴外
部融資、尚未形成穩定獲利模式的基礎建設業者，對資金成本及監管變化的承受能力
並不相同。未來市場可能更加重視個別企業的獲利品質，而非單純以AI 相關營收成長
作為估值依據。
2. 政治事件可能延長科技股評價調整，但中期走勢仍取決於企業獲利：本次期中選舉引
發的期貨跌勢，反映市場對政策方向及國會控制權變化的即時反應，惟政治不確定性
通常須透過實際立法、企業投資計畫或經濟活動變化，才會形成較持久的基本面影響
。從企業獲利角度觀察，路透社引用 LSEG 資料指出，S&P 500 第三季企業獲利仍呈
現強勁成長，科技企業對整體獲利貢獻顯著，顯示目前尚無充分證據支持美國企業基
本面全面轉弱。不過，科技股先前已累積較高成長預期，市場對資本支出回報及監管
風險的容忍度降低，可能使股價即使在營收成長下仍承受評價壓力。短期而言，若選
舉結果逐漸明朗，且企業獲利預估未進一步下修，市場有機會逐步消化政治風險；反
之，若政策不確定性伴隨獲利預期下修，修正時間可能延長。

不另行通知。
3. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：根據彭博統計，1950
年以來美國歷次期中選舉後 12 個月，S&P 500 平均報酬率約 15%，且 19 次全數
收正。其中最高為 1954 年的逾 30%，表現最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。我們認為
，選前政策不確定性消除加上府會分治抑制財政與監管的轉向是為歷史上選後行情的
主要支撐，但相關數據所呈現的為過往經驗，未來股市表現能否延續先前情況仍應觀
察後續基本面變化。
4. 美國公債具避險價值，但利率下行能否持續仍取決於通膨與財政前景：在美國期中選

舉開票期間，美股期貨下跌伴隨美債殖利率回落，顯示公債仍能在股市波動期間提供
一定的風險分散效果。然而，本次殖利率下降可能同時包含避險買盤、財政刺激預期
下修及市場對經濟成長前景重新評估等因素，不宜直接解讀為聯準會即將轉向寬鬆。
尤其能源市場仍受到中東地緣政治及供應中斷風險影響，油價波動可能使通膨前景反
覆，限制長天期公債殖利率的下行空間。若未來財政擴張受到限制，可能對長端利率
形成部分支撐；但若政治僵局提高債務上限協商風險，亦可能使美債期限溢酬波動。
因此，債券配置仍須同時考量收益率、存續期間與通膨風險，不宜僅根據單日行情決
定配置方向。
5. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：目前科技股修正主
要反映評價與政策風險，但若AI 企業持續增加資本支出及外部融資，市場對債務負擔
與償債能力的關注可能進一步升高。對大型投資等級科技企業而言，較穩定的營運現
金流及融資管道有助於吸收資本支出壓力；但對依賴單一客戶合約、設備抵押融資或
高槓桿經營的 AI 基礎建設業者 ， 利率與信用利差上升可能更直接影響投資回報及再融

資能力。後續應追蹤投資等級債與高收益債 OAS、科技及雲端相關發行人利差、新債
發行需求及信用評等展望。若科技股下跌但整體信用利差維持穩定，市場風險可能仍
以股權評價調整為主；若信用利差同步持續擴大，則須提高對金融條件收緊及風險跨
市場擴散的警覺。

四、 投資建議：
1. 科技股中期成長趨勢仍具支撐，惟短線宜審慎布局並重視個股分化：美國期中選舉帶
來的政策不確定性，短期可能使科技股評價承壓，惟大型雲端業者持續投入AI 基礎建
設，半導體及相關設備需求仍具中期成長動能，目前尚不宜因美股期貨在開票期間的
下挫即全面轉向保守。我們仍建議維持美股核心配置，但短期避免過度集中於高評價
、高資本支出及高度依賴外部融資的企業。產業方面，資訊科技維持選擇性配置，優
先關注具備技術競爭優勢、穩定營運現金流及較高投資回報能見度的企業；對雲端基
礎建設及 AI 融資相關標的，則應提高財務結構與現金流審查標準。

2. 維持中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債配置，兼顧收益與風險分散：在美國政策風
險升溫及科技股評價調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源
價格的不確定性，現階段不建議延長存續期間。建議以中天期美國公債及高信評投資
等級債作為主要配置方向，兼顧票息收益、利率敏感度與資產配置穩定性。信用債方
面，建議優先選擇營運現金流充足、槓桿適中及再融資需求可控的發行人，對AI 相關
企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合約現金流與債務到期結構。

NYPCB (8046.TW)

NYPCB
Valuation
Our DCF-based TP for NYPCB is NT$1,700. With a risk-free rate of 5.00% (10-year government bond rate), a market risk
premium of 7% and an equity beta of 1.22 (YTD weekly share price), we calculate NYPCB's WACC as 13.4%. Our TP is equivalent
to 30x/13x 2027E EPS/BVPS, which reflects its high P/E multiple during upcycles. NYPCB traded at 15-20x P/E during its
2003-2008 upcycle driven by PC/NB demand strength. In 2010-11 when the market was recovering from the global financial

crisis, NYPCB traded at 25-35x P/E. In the last 2019-22 upcycle when ABF was in shortage, NYPCB traded at 30-40x P/E.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the NYPCB shares from reaching our target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected ABF
demand from key customers; 2) BT business seasonality weakness and fewer-than-expected price hikes; and 3) severe T glass
shortages that cause a bottleneck to its production.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day

15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

### Page 1

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
3006 晶豪科(ESMT)

報告日期： 2026/11/03 研 究 員：王蕎程  (Joe Wang)  
壹、訪談／法說內容 
重點摘要: 
 記憶體漲價帶動獲利： 3Q26 營收 221.50 億元， QoQ 
+58.83% ，毛 利 率77.44%；預 估4Q26 營收 239.22 億元 ，QoQ 
+8.0% ，稅後 EPS 39.50 元。  
 成熟記憶體供給吃緊： DDR2 、DDR3 合計營收占比逾五成，
受惠同業減供及客戶轉單，加上高階、商務應用需求暢旺，支
撐產品價格維持高檔。  
 預估 2026 ／2027 年稅後 EPS 102.33 ／159.38 元：受惠合約
價格調升及低基期，預估全年營收分別為 672.85 ／1,019.90
億元， YoY +361.6% ／+51.6% 。

一. 3Q26 稅後 EPS 36.79 元，漲價帶動毛利率升  
晶豪科主要從事記憶體 IC 設計，產品涵蓋 DRAM、Flash、MCP 、
eMCP 及 eMMC ，今年記憶體產品營收比重約 95% 。受惠記憶體
供給吃緊、合約價格逐季調升， 3Q26 營收 221.50 億元， QoQ 
+58.83% 、YoY +519.22% ；毛利率 77.44% ，較上季增加 8.74 個
百分點；營業利益 131.50 億元， QoQ +83.33% ，營業利益率
59.37% ； 稅後純益107.59 億元 ，QoQ +82.20% ，稅 後EPS 36.79
元。營收及獲利成長主要來自產品價格上漲，出貨量受限於合作
晶圓廠產能，增幅相對有限。

二、 DDR2 、DDR3 營收占比五成，供給收縮支撐價格  
隨記憶體大廠將資源轉向 DDR4 、DDR5 及 HBM ，成熟記憶體供
給減少，缺貨情況逐步擴及 DDR3 、DDR2 及 Flash 。公司表示，
國內同業停止或減少供應 DDR2 、DDR3 ，帶動客戶轉單，第三季
兩項產品合計營收占比已超過 50% 。需求方面， PC、NB 及手機
因應 AI 應用提高記憶體搭載容量，加上 AI 基礎架構、周邊設備
與邊緣運算需求增加，有助帶動非 HBM 記憶體需求。儘管部分消
費性客戶因價格漲幅較大而觀望，高階及商務應用拉貨仍暢旺，
支撐公司接單表現。

展望第四季，公司表示產品價格將高於第三季，出貨量則預期與
第三季相近。考量合約價格續升，預估 4Q26 營收 239.22 億元，
QoQ 8.0% ；毛利率 78.44% ，營業利益 144.40 億元，稅後純益

四、預估 2026 ／2027 年稅後 EPS 102.33 ／159.38 元 
預估 2026 全年營收 672.85 億元 ，YoY +361.6% ；營 業 毛 利489.51
億元，毛利率 72.75% ；營業利益 370.17 億元，稅後純益 305.07
億元，稅後 EPS 102.33 元。展望 2027 年，受惠成熟記憶體供給
受限、高階及商務應用需求支撐，預期產品價格維持高檔，搭配

YoY +51.6% ；營業毛利 764.21 億元，毛利率 74.93% ；營業利益
588.51 億元，稅後純益 475.16 億元， YoY +55.8% ，稅後 EPS 
159.38 元。

五. 投資建議買進，目標價 320 元 
考量 AI 趨勢下帶動原廠將產能轉做 HBM，以 及DDR 4 轉入 DDR 
5，帶動利基型記憶體市場正向效益，記憶體供不應求的現象持
續，帶動價格持續大幅上升，使晶豪科營收獲利自 4Q25 開始明
顯跳升， 2027 年全年供需缺口無解，價格上升趨勢趨緩但仍有上
行空間，帶動晶豪科獲利維持高檔，因此，投資建議買進，目標
價 320 元(2X2027 年稅後 EPS) 。

貳、公 司 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
晶豪科 (3006) 成立於 1998 年 ， 公司主要以提供各類型記憶體產品
及技術為主。目前記憶體佔營收比重約 90% ，Audio 佔 10% ，其
中特定型 DRAM 產品廣泛應用於 PC 週邊 、 資訊家電 、 及消費性、
通訊等系統。公司已成功建立各種容量及介面規格的特定型
DRAM 產品線 (包括 SDRAM 、DDR I/II/III 及 PSRAM 、低耗電之
Mobile DRAM 等)；在快閃記憶體方面，亦已完成多種容量及介面
類型的 NOR Flash 及 NAND Flash 的開發，可滿足各種特定應用
系統的需求。另外，由於系統級封裝 (SiP) 之需求快速增加，晶豪
科技也已經成功開發上述各項產品所需之「良品晶粒」
(Known-Good-Die;KGD) 產品及多晶片模組封裝 (MCP) 的解決方
案，以滿足客戶的各類需求。尤於晶豪科記憶體產品大部份以利
基型記憶體為主，受標準型記憶體價格波動干擾程度相對較低，
有助維持穩定毛利率。

二.營運概況及展望

1、IoT 應用持續成長，為重要成長動能 
5G 於 2H19 正式商轉，隨著基礎建設逐漸完善，連網速度及穩定
度成熟， 2021 年進入大幅應用階段，除了 5G 智慧型手機需求量
將從 2020 年的 2～2.5 億支倍數成長至 4.5 ～5 億支之外，因 5G
高頻、高速、廣連結的特色，連網速度將較 4G 時代大幅提升 10
～100 倍，將推升物聯網 (IoT) 應用快速成長，預估 5G 同時連網
裝置可達 600 ～800 億個，較 4G 時代的 100 億個大幅成長。看好
未來物聯網市場將蓬勃發展 ， 晶豪科於2020 年新成立物聯網解決
方案部門，提供客戶 RF、Wireless 的 SoC IC 及模組提供低功率
網際網路進行裝置連結，目前使用 90nm 製程節點，未來將進入
到 55nm 。2020 年新成立的物聯網解決方案部門站營收比重僅
<0.5% ，但因全球物聯網發展將因 5G 建設而蓬勃發展，預估繼
2022 年逆勢成長後， 2023 年可望延續此一成長動能。

IOT 為晶豪科近年延伸觸角的重要產品 。 IOT 將建構於 5G 佈建的
完整性， 2020 年為 5G 建置元年， 2021 年將加速建置，加速 IOT
產業發展。由於大部份 IOT 產品僅需要 DDR 3 規格即可，且全球
主要記憶體廠均優先佈局以 Server 使用的 DDR 5 為主，因此，
預估未來 DDR 3 將保持穩定的需求量。晶豪科將以無線模組切入
中國智慧電表領域，及其他各類相關 IOT 產品，為未來營運成長
重要動能。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 3Q26 稅後 EPS 36.79 元，漲價帶動毛利率升 
展望第四季，公司表示產品價格高於第三季，出貨量則預期與第
三季相近 。 考量合約價格續升 ， 預估4Q26 營收 239.22 億元 ，QoQ 
+8.0%；毛 利 率78.44%， 營業利益144.40 億元 ， 稅後純益117.76
億元， EPS 39.50 元。惟晶圓代工及封裝測試成本逐步增加，後
續仍須關注售價調升與成本上漲的相對幅度。新產品方面，公司
針對邊緣 AI 運算開發 aiPIM 加速器記憶體，第三季已完成
tape-out ，後續關注晶片驗證、客戶導入及量產進度，短期營運仍
以既有記憶體產品為主要動能。。

- 1 - 
個股報告 
富世達(6805) 
伺服器占比突破八成，QD量價齊揚推升獲利 
投資評等 目標價 
買進 $3000 
前次投資建議 2026.08.05: 
「買進」 ，目標價: $2360

報告日期：2026.11.04 研 究 員：陳威仲

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q3 毛利率優於預期，伺服器產品營收占比升至 81.3%，上修 2026/27 年 EPS 至
65.5/120.1 元(前次預估 64.3/112.8元)， 目標價由2360 元調升至 3000 元(25X 27(F)PER)，
維持【買進】投資評等。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/3 收盤價： 2160.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 45.0 億元 (QoQ+20.4%/YoY+26.2%)，受
VR200、ASIC 平台小量拉貨與非奇鋐之快接頭客戶出貨
增加帶動；毛利率 38.0%(季增 3.9ppts/年增 10.3ppts，
優於預期 2.9ppts)，主因伺服器產品營收占比升至
81.3%；營業淨利 14.6 億元(QoQ+39.9%/YoY+84.2%)；
稅後淨利 12.4億元(QoQ+46.3%/YoY+80.8%)，EPS 18.1
元。 
➢ QD 受惠 GPU 與 ASIC 雙平台放量及規格升級，Rubin 
Ultra 將導入單價約兩倍的 ZQD，AWS Trn3 等 ASIC 專
案將於 2027 年陸續貢獻；AI 滑軌亦陸續通過驗證，27
年可望隨 VR200與 ASIC專案放量。 
➢ 華為摺疊機新品拉貨遞延，加上記憶體漲價影響終端需
求 ， 下 修26Q4 營 收 預 估 至54.0 億元
(QoQ+20.0%/YoY+35.8%)；惟伺服器產品比重上升，毛
利率上修至 38.1%，EPS 21.63元。 
➢ 預估 2026/27 年 EPS 65.5/120.1 元，將持續受惠強勁的
伺服器需求與水冷滲透率提升之下帶動獲利成長，目前股
價分別位於 2026/27 年 EPS的 33.0/18.0X，過去股價區
間落在 15~87X，目標價 3000 元(25X 27(F)PER)，維持
【買進】投資評等，潛在上漲空間 38.9%。

營 運 分 析

富世達為 AI基礎設施與高階摺疊機轉軸的關鍵供應商 
富世達股份有限公司成立於民國 2001 年，為奇鋐(3017)持股 16.87%的轉投
資公司，總部與研發中心位於新北新莊，並於越南、中國深圳設有生產基地，
提供各類金屬沖壓設計與製造，主要產品為伺服器組件(滑軌、水冷快接頭)，
與摺疊手機、筆記型電腦、平板電腦之轉軸零件，從消費性電子零組件廠轉
型為 AI基礎設施與高階摺疊機轉軸的關鍵供應商，主要出貨終端包含華為、
Motorola 等摺疊機，與各大AI伺服器平台(NVIDIA、AMD、ASIC等)機櫃用
的滑軌與水冷快接頭。

資料來源：富世達、兆豐投顧整理與預估

26Q3 伺服器產品營收占比上升至 81.3%，帶動毛利率大幅優於預期。 
富世達 26Q3 營收 45.0 億元(QoQ+20.4%/YoY+26.2%，優於兆豐前次預期
4%)，因 VR200、ASIC 平台小量拉貨與非奇鋐之快接頭客戶規模上升使伺
服器快接頭營收增長強勁；毛利率 38.0%(季增 3.9ppts/年增 10.3ppts，優於
兆豐預期 2.9ppts)，主因產品組合改善，伺服器產品營收占比超越 80%；營
業淨利 14.6 億元 (QoQ+39.9%/YoY+84.2%)， 稅 後 淨 利12.4 億元
(QoQ+46.3%/YoY+80.8%)，EPS 18.1元(前次預估 15.9 元)。

資料來源：Cmoney、兆豐投顧整理與預估

QD 受惠 GPU與 ASIC雙平台放量及規格升級，為伺服器產品主要成長動能 
26Q3 富世達伺服器產品占營收 81.3%，其中快接頭(QD)約 90%，整體單季
稼動率約 80~90%；而目前新產能開出後 QD 產能已達 1350 萬顆/季。今年
QD 營收貢獻以 GPU 平台為主，明年 ASIC 比重將持續提高，預期 2028 年
ASIC 比重有望超越 GPU；其中(1)AWS Trn3 已配合客戶時程出貨，將於
27H1顯著貢獻營收。(2)Google TPU V9世代有望由現行的 NSP1接頭，變
為導入混用 QD+NSP1接頭，以優化未來光模塊、DIMM 等部件潛在導入水
冷系統後維護的容易性。(3)Meta MTIA 與微軟 MAIA 等 AISC 專案也將於
27H2 出貨帶動 QD 市場規模提升。而在 Rubin Ultra 世代，將導入 ZQD 於
運算托盤(取代部份的 UQD)與 rack manifold之快接頭，且ZQD單顆價值預
估將為現行 MQD與 UQD 單價的兩倍，ZQD 預計 12月進入量產準備。 
規格面，VR200 單櫃 QD 數量約 538 顆(含 rack manifold 88 顆)，Rubin 
Ultra(VR300)將為 574~664顆，預估 QD量價齊揚趨勢延續，為2027~2028
年主要成長動能。

AI 滑軌陸續通過驗證，27年可望隨 VR200與 ASIC 專案量產放量 
滑軌事業目前月產能維持 35 萬套，目前以通用型為主，稼動率約 3 成；隨
明年 AI專案的量產，稼動率可望提升至50~60%以上，毛利率也將隨著 ASIC
水冷專案的放量同步改善，水冷櫃滑軌對承重、緩衝與平行度要求較高，ASP
亦較高，公司預期明年滑軌維持高度成長，後續將視訂單規劃擴廠。

- 4 - 
個股報告

華為摺疊機新品拉貨遞延與記憶體推升價格，轉軸營收短期承壓 
華為新機種受記憶體影響拉貨延後，加上終端需求轉弱，26Q3富世達手機
軸承營收占比降至 15.5%，預期26Q4手機轉軸拉貨動能有限。而華為 26H1
中國摺疊機市占逾 7成，摺疊機累計出貨突破 2,000 萬支；惟記憶體漲價使
單機平均成本增加約 200 美元，9/1 起主力機型最高調漲 17%，後續產品將
朝輕薄化發展並導入新材料，每代價格將重新議價，下一代機種仍由富世達
主供。雖蘋果 iPhone Duo 上市帶動摺疊機話題，將使摺疊機長期接受度與
滲透率同步提高，但短期消費性電子仍受記憶體價格推升單價而影響終端購
買意願。

26Q4 營收下修、毛利率上修，獲利預估小幅調降 
預估富世達 26Q4 營收 54.0 億元(QoQ+20.0%/YoY+35.8%，較原估下修
8.2%，主因摺疊機軸承拉貨不如預期 )；毛利率 38.1%(季增 0.1ppts/年增
9.0ppts，較原估上修 1.1ppts，主因 QD 生產進入規模化，帶動高毛利伺服
器比重上升)；稅後淨利 14.8 億元(QoQ+19.4%/YoY+97.3%，較原估下修
4.7%)，EPS 21.63元(原估 22.7 元)。

富世達將持續受惠強勁的伺服器需求與水冷滲透率提升之下帶動獲利成長 
預估 2026/27 年 EPS 65.5/120.1元(前次預估 64.3/112.8元)，預估在多項
AI伺服器專案導入水冷，與後續電源、光模組等部件皆將導入 100%水冷，
2027~2028 年水冷散熱滲透率將大幅上升，帶動富世達伺服器營收持續增長 
，目前股價分別位於 2026/27 年 EPS的 33.0/18.0X，過去股價區間落在
15~87X，目標價由 2360 元(25X 27(F)PER)調整至 3000 元(25X 
27(F)PER)，維持【買進】投資評等，潛在上漲空間 38.9%。

### Page 1

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Earnings Review Company Analysis | 이차전지/화학 | 2026. 11. 4  
일회성 보상금 포함된 실적, ‘30년 ESS 수요 대비 배 이상 높은 셀 3사 Capa 우려

AMPC 4,169억원 포함 영업이익 컨센서스 대폭 상회 
동사는 8일 매출액 9조 6,434억원(YoY 59%, QoQ 27%), AMPC 포함한 영업이익 
7,560억원(OPM 7.8%, YoY 26%, QoQ 567%)로 시장 Consensus(공정공시한 8일 
기준 3,264억원)을 대폭 상회하는 어닝 서프라이즈 발표. 회사는 ESS 출하 급증과 고
전압 Mid-Ni EV 파우치 확대, 견조한 EV향 2170-46시리즈 공급을 물량 중심을 성
장하였고, 폴란드 가동률 개선, 수익성이 견조한 원통형 물량 증가, 북미 ESS 생산성 
개선에 따른 고정비 부담 완화 및 고객사의 일회성 물량 보상금 영향으로 설명. 그러나 
일회성 물량 보상금의 구체적 금액, 반복 발생 여부는 제시하지 않아 불확실성 남겨. 
7,560억원의 영업이익에도 불구하고 영업외손실 9,872억원 발생으로 당기순손실 기
록. 이에는 소형 IT파우치 자산손상 반영되어, 가동률 개선 및 감가상각비 감소를 위해 
Capa를 조정하는 배터리 회사들의 대응 전략이 이번 실적에서 다시 확인

‘30년 ESS 수요 전망들 대비 국내사 Capa 배 이상 높아 
동사는 북미 ESS Capa 확대를 통한 탈중국 수요에 적극 대응하겠다는 전략. 파우치 
라인 추가 전환 및 신규 각형 라인 확보를 통해 북미 ESS Capa를 ‘26년말 50+에서 
‘27년 70+로 제시. 최근 타사에서 ‘26년 미국 ESS 수요를 80~100GWh, 혹은 
130+GWh로, ‘30년 두배 이상, 혹은 240+GWh로 수요 전망에 대해 답변한 바 있음. 
동사 전체 Capa 300GWh(‘25년 연말), SK On 179GWh, SDI 140GWh)임을 감안하
면 ‘30년 약 2배 수요를 가정하면 셀 3사 Capa의 32%에 달하는 수요, 만일 3배 수
요 가정하면 48.5% 수준. 북미 외 시장 가성비 중시 기조 및 중국 LFP 장악(‘25년 
97% M/S)으로 진입장벽 높음을 감안하면 미국 수요 중요. 이를 상쇄하기 위해 EV향 
배터리의 안정적인 공급 지속 필요하며, 이를 위해 동사 원통형은 TESLA의 비미국 시
장 판매량이, 파우치는 시장 자체 축소 지속 여부에 유의할 필요 
상대적으로 가장 견조한 출하량, 그러나 고평가 영역  
셀3사중 EV향 배터리 출하량 및 ESS 탑재에서 현재 가장 견조한 판매량을 보이고 있
으나, AMPC와 일회성 보상금이 영업손익의 상당부분을 견인한다는 점에서 수익성 취
약하다는 단점. 또한 ROE가 (-) 영역 임에도 PBR이 3.x내외로 거래되고 있어 고평가 
지적에서 자유롭기 어려워. 당분간 모회사인 LG화학의 PRS 발행에 따른 단기 오버행 
이슈도 관건. PBR 2.3 x(‘25년대비 하락하는 ROE 감안, 20% 할인)적용 목표주가 
290,000원 제시, HOLD 유지. 주식 투자 매력 낮은 상황으로 판단 
 Analyst 정경희 
khchung@ls-sec.co.kr

### Page 2

Company Report / LG에너지솔루션 / 2026. 11.4 
LS Securities Research 2 
EV향 3분기 data  
이차전지에서 EV향 비중은 여전히 가장 높다. 타입별로 보면 3분기 글로벌 EV향 이차전
지에서 각형의 M/S가 83%로 지속 증가하고 있으며, 동사가 생산하는 원통형과 파우치의 
경우 합산 M/S가 17%로 하락하였다. 현재 Wh 기준 동사 EV향 배터리에서 3분기 파우
치가 55%, 원통형이 45%로 파우치 비중이 10% 원통형을 상회한다. 
그림1 LGES 원통형, 파우치 타입 EV 향 분기별 탑재량 추이 
그림2

자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터

그림 2 EV 향 폼팩터 M/S: 각형 확장 vs 원통형/파우치 감소  그림 3 동사 EV 배터리 타입 비중

자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터  자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터

SNE data에 따르면 글로벌 원통형 EV향 수요의 대다수가 TESLA에서 발생하고 있으며, 
‘24~’25년 역성장후 ‘26년 판매량 회복을 나타내는 TESLA 호조로 전년대비 LGES의 EV
향 원통형 판매량은 3분기 증가했다. 반면 파우치의 경우 ‘25년 파우치 탑재 EV 판매 호조
를 나타낸 현대기아차그룹 영향으로 YoY 탑재량이 증가했으나, 올해는 북미 OEM인 GM, 
Ford향 판매가 IRA 일몰로 전년대비 부진함에 따라 3분기 전년대비 25% 감소한 것으로 
나타난다.

그림 9 셀 3 사 분기별 EV 향 탑재량 글로벌 M/S 추이: M/S 감소중

자료: SNE 리서치, LS증권 리서치센터

밸류에이션, 수익성 대비 고평가 영역  
동사는 ‘24년 ROE -4.9%, ‘25년 -5.2%에 이어 ‘26년 -4.4%, ‘27년 -3.4%로 (-) 영역이 
지속되는 것으로 예상한다. ‘24년과 ‘25년 순이익은 (+)지만, 해외 합작회사가 많고 이들 이
익을 연결함에 따라 지배주주순이익은 적자를 기록한 것을 보인다. JV 규모가 축소되고 있
어 이러한 순이익과 지배주주 순이익상의 괴리는 좁혀지겠지만, ROE가 (-)영역 지속을 예
상함에 따라 현재 거래 PBR은 고평가 상황으로 볼 수 있다. 물론 이것은 향후 수익 성장
에 대한 시장 기대가 반영된 것으로 볼 수 있지만, 이차전지 6개사 평균 Consensus 대비 
실제 수익이 ‘23년부터 지속 하향되고 있다는 점, ESS대비 여전히 판매비중이 높은 EV에서 
출하량 정체가 일어나고 있다는 점, 미국 ESS에서 여전히 높은 중국 M/S를 감안하면 이러
한 기대 수준에 대해 다시 검토할 필요가 있다. 이에 더해, 모회사 기준에서는 필요한 과정
이지만 동사 주가 측면에서는 LG화학의 PRS 발행도 단기 오버행으로 당분간 주가에 부담
을 줄 수 있다.

그림 10 셀 3 사, 양∙음극재 3 사 추정치 변화율: ‘23 년부터 추정치 지속 하향 조정

자료: DataGuide, LS증권 리서치센터

미국 ESS가 모든 것을 해결할 수 있나? 
‘25년 글로벌 ESS 시장의 97%를 중국배터리사가 공급했다. LFP 각형이 표준화된 시장인 
것이다. 미국 시장만 중국에 대한 관세 허들, 정책 허들로 한국 배터리사에 기회로 작용한
다. 그러나 ‘26년부터 시작된 FEOC와 관세추가 부담에도 불구, 상반기 미국 ESS 시장의 
76%을 (기타사의 국적을 알 수 없기에 제외하더라도) 중국 셀사가 차지했다. 만일 한국 배
터리 유관회사들이 전망하듯 ‘30년 미국 ESS 시장이 160~200GWh 이상, 240+GWh, 혹
은 240~300GWh가 된다고 해도 3사 전체 Capa의 50%가 채 되지 못한다. EV 수요가 뒷
받침되지 않는다면 미국 ESS만으로는 가동률을 80~90% 이상 올리기 어렵다는 의미다. 특
히 동사는 원통형과 파우치를 생산하고 있으나, 원통형은 북미외 지역 TESLA의 판매에, 
파우치는 올해 출하량이 감소하는 것으로 나타나고 있다. 파우치 고객의 각형 채택 비중 
증가를 감안하면 동사 EV향 배터리 출하량 증가를 확신하기는 어렵다.   
긍정적 기대와 동시에 냉정한 점검이 필요한 시점으로 판단한다.

LS Securities Research 6 
표2 LG 에너지솔루션 3Q26 실적발표 주요 Q&A 
[Q&A]           
Q1-1. 4Q26 실적 전망과 2027년 큰 방향은? 
  A. 4Q26 ESS 출하량은 북미 수요 대응으로 QoQ 약 40% 증가 예상 
   유럽 고전압 Mid-Ni 파우치 및 원통형 물량도 연말까지 견조한 출하 지속 전망 
   3Q26 가동을 시작한 북미 JV 효과가 더해져 전사 매출은 QoQ 약 10% 성장 가능할 것으로 예상 
   가동률 개선과 사이트 안정적 램프업은 지속 추진하나 3Q26 일회성 요인 소멸의 기저효과 존재 
   4Q26 혼다 JV ESS CAPA 추가 가동 준비에 따른 초기 고정비 부담으로 수익성 일부 둔화 불가피

2027년 사업계획은 수립 중이며 전방 수요 변수로 구체적 가이던스 제시는 어려운 단계 
    ESS: 북미 확보 수주 기반으로 유의미한 물량 성장 지속 기대 
    소형: 전략 고객향 EV 원통형과 46 시리즈 신규 애플리케이션 판매로 안정적 성장 예상 
    EV 파우치: 중저가 제품 물량과 북미 EV 향 생산품이 더해져 성장 예상 
   전 사업부 매출 성장 기대하나 핵심 제품 경쟁력·기존 CAPA 효율화·가동률 극대화·원가 개선에 집중 
   2027년 매출 성장률·영업이익률 등 구체적 수치는 제시 X

Q1-2. 북미 PFE 요건 충족을 위한 LFP 공급망 전략과 중국 관련 주력 양극재 공급사 대응은? 
  A. 미국 생산세액공제 확보를 위해 직접재료비 내 PFE 소재 비중 요건을 충족하는 공급망 구축 필요 
   회사 설명상 PFE 소재 허용 비중은 2026년 40%에서 2027년 35% 이하로 강화. Non-PFE 비중과 혼동 유의 
   소재별 소싱 전략을 구체화해 준비 중이며 요건 충족은 차질 없이 진행될 것으로 판단 
   재료비 비중이 큰 양극재는 최근 계약한 국내 업체 외 복수 Non-PFE 업체 조달 옵션 확보

음극재·분리막·전해액도 기존 Non-PFE 공급망에 더해 신규 업체 적극 발굴 
   기존 원가 중심 최적화에서 정책·지정학 리스크를 고려한 공급망 안정성 강화로 전략 확대 
   협력사의 원가·기술·물량 대응력을 종합 평가하고 장기적으로 현지 협력사 발굴·현지화 추진 
   질문한 중국 관련 주력 공급사의 개별 지위·조달 비중에 대한 직접적인 상세 답변 X

Q2-1. ESS 사업의 수익성 전망 및 개선 방안은? 
  A. EV→ESS 라인 전환으로 수요에 맞는 생산체계를 구축하고 북미 고객 적기 공급에 주력 
   다수 사이트 동시 램프업에 어려움이 있었고 고정비 절감 노력에도 수익성 개선 속도는 시장 기대보다 느렸음 
   2026년 말 50GWh 목표 셀 라인 램프업은 마무리 국면. Link CAPA도 3Q26 에 이어 4Q26 추가 보강 예정 
   CAPA 안정화와 점진적인 고정비 부담 완화 기대 
   확보 CAPA 효율화, 소재 소싱 다변화로 원재료비·물류비 절감에 집중 
   AI 데이터센터 및 SI 프로젝트 비중 확대를 통한 고객 포트폴리오 최적화 추진 
   셀·시스템 고용량화로 원가 경쟁력을 높여 구조적 수익성 개선 추진 
   구체적 ESS 영업이익률 목표나 개선 폭은 제시 X

Q2-2. 북미 ESS 추가 수주 현황과 향후 모멘텀은? 
  A. 빠른 시장 성장 및 Non-PFE 에 우호적인 정책으로 북미 현지 생산 셀 수요 급증 
   현재 계획된 CAPA 기준으로 2028년까지 물량은 대부분 확정. 그 이후 물량 중심으로 고객 수주 협의 진행 
   기존 전력망·유틸리티 고객, 이들을 통한 AI 데이터센터 BESS 수요, 하이퍼스케일러 BTM 직접 구매 수요 포함 
   예상 수요가 공급 가능 CAPA를 초과해 전략적으로 중요한 프로젝트 중심으로 미래 잔여 CAPA 운영 계획 
   추가 고객명·신규 계약량 등은 제시하지 않음. 예상 수요와 확정 수주를 구분

Q3-1. 46시리즈의 구체적인 수주 현황과 향후 모멘텀은? 
  A. 기존 OEM 부터 EV 스타트업까지 이미 440GWh 이상 물량 확보 
   추가로 다수 글로벌 OEM 과 협의 중이며 가까운 시일 내 의미 있는 규모의 수주 성과 기대 
   제품 경쟁력·지역별 생산 역량으로 46 시리즈 선도 입지를 강화하고 고객 포트폴리오 확대 계획 
   440GWh는 확보 물량이며 연간 판매량이나 생산 CAPA가 아님. 추가 계약 규모·고객명은 미공개

### Page 7

Company Report / LG에너지솔루션 / 2026. 11.4 
LS Securities Research 7

Q3-2. Tabless 2170의 경쟁력과 수주·신규 시장 대응 현황은? 
  A. 기존 제품 대비 낮은 발열·저항으로 안전성 및 고출력에 유리해 HEV·BBU·로봇 등 다양한 용도에 적합 
   오창에서 전동공구용 제품은 이미 양산 중  
   BBU 향 LFP 2170은 2Q27 양산 목표로 준비 중 
   HEV 향은 2027년 말 애리조나에서 양산 계획  
   LFP 2170은 NCM 대비 열안전성 테스트에서 우수한 성능을 보여 고객의 긍정적 피드백 확보 
   고객 니즈·수요에 적극 대응해 신시장 경쟁력 확보 추진. 수주 규모 등 정량 정보는 제시하지 않음

Q4-1. 재무 건전성 개선 방안과 2027 년 자금 조달 계획은? 
  A. 선제 CAPA 투자 자금은 저리 정책자금 장기차입 및 원화·외화 회사채로 안정적으로 조달해 왔음 
   주요 투자는 대부분 집행됐고 CAPEX는 2025년을 기점으로 유의미하게 감소 중 
   향후 영업현금흐름 확대분을 차입금 상환에 우선 활용, 자산 유동화를 통한 추가 재원 확보도 병행 
   차입금·금융비용 부담을 축소해 재무지표 개선 및 건전성 확보 추진 
   2026년 본사 추가 차입 계획은 없으며 해외법인은 기존 약정 차입금 일부 추가 인출 예정 
   2027년은 기존 차입금 차환·운전자금 범위에서 최소한의 자금 조달 계획 
   영업현금 확대·투자 부담 완화에 따른 잉여 재원으로 차입금 감축 기조 지속. 구체적 조달 규모는 미제시

Q4-2. 유럽 IAA 정책 방향에 대응한 중장기 점유율 확보 전략은? 
  A. IAA는 아직 최종안 미확정이나 수정 권고안상 외국인 직접투자·현지화 요건 강화 예상 
   배터리뿐 아니라 주요 소재의 현지 생산 역량 중요성이 확대될 전망 
   유럽 OEM 의 공급망 다변화로 현지 생산 체계를 갖춘 비중국 업체 소싱 니즈 확대, 경쟁 환경은 우호적 판단 
   폴란드 현지 양산 및 소재·부품 공급망을 이미 구축해 현지 배터리 소싱 수요에 신속 대응 가능 
   고전압 Mid-Ni 에 대한 유럽 고객 관심 증가. 폴란드 감가상각비 감소도 가격 경쟁력 확보에 기여 
   고전압 Mid-Ni 외 LFP·CTP 등 다양한 제품·솔루션으로 신규 수주를 확보해 유럽 점유율 확대 추진

자료: LS증권 리서치센터

Document 310: Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 mi

Source PDF: all report pdf/Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 min read.pdf

### Page 1

13028181     1

Economy 
Business Checkpoint: Looking into the logistics 
15 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Small and mid-sized businesses entered Q3 in relatively solid financial condition, with profitability growth up across both 
groups, according to Bank of America business account data. Nearly half of estimated tariff payments had been refunded by 
September, though the share of these refunds relative to revenue has peaked. 
• In Bank of America business payments data, logistics spending growth per client was up four times as much for small than mid-
sized firms. Additionally, distribution and inventory transactions growth per client has outpaced spending since the spring of 
2025. 
• Growing transportation bottlenecks are re-emerging ahead of the holiday season. Trucking capacity has tightened as driver 
availability remains constrained, according to BofA Global Research. This has pushed freight rates higher and driven strong 
transportation sector payroll growth, particularly among mid-sized businesses, according to Bank of America payments data.

Business Checkpoint is a new, regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It provides a real-time view of the financial health, opportunities 
and challenges facing small and mid-sized businesses across the US economy, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America's proprietary 
data. By analyzing activity among both small and mid-sized firms, Business Checkpoint offers timely insights into business profitability, hiring, 
investment and spending trends. Such data is not intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, 
financial condition or performance of Bank of America. 
Businesses end Q3 in fair shape 
While profitability growth remained positive for both small and mid-sized firms in the third quarter, small firms outpaced their 
mid-sized counterparts for the second consecutive quarter (Exhibit 2). Still, there’s been a notable reversal in the profitability of 
mid-sized businesses, growing this quarter after declining slightly for the past two.  
The continued rollout of tariff refunds is serving as a tailwind to some firms' cash positions. That is particularly true for small 
firms. According to Bank of America account data, by September approximately 47% of estimated International Emergency 
Economic Powers Act-related tariff payments had been refunded to companies with less than $50 million in annual revenue.  
Exhibit 1: Tariff refunds as a share of income for businesses <$50M 
in annual revenue peaked in June

  I NSTI TUTE

According to Bank of America business payments data, the percentage point difference in tariff refunds as a share of revenue 
for firms with less than $50 million in annual revenue spiked in June but dropped to nearly zero in September. At the same time, 
the percentage point difference in tariff payments as a share of expenses began rising again relative to the 2023 average 
(Exhibit 1). This suggests that while tariffs continue to affect companies, the refund program has provided temporary relief for 
some businesses.  
Wholesale trade along with transportation and warehousing posted solid profitability growth across business size 
However, the benefits appear uneven. Such refunds have boosted cash flow, but they have not fully insulated firms from broader 
cost pressures.  
Looking across industries, mid-sized construction and wholesale firms had the strongest profitability growth in September 
(Exhibit 3). Notably, transportation and warehousing, as well as wholesale trade were among the few sectors showing positive 
profitability growth for both small and mid-sized firms last month. 
The persistence of logistics, freight and inventory-related cost pressures may help explain why profitability gains have remained 
relatively concentrated in transportation and wholesale trade. However, these sectors also have a higher exposure to rising gas 
prices within their margins (read more on this in the August Small Business Checkpoint). 
Exhibit 3: For both small and mid-sized businesses, profitability growth was positive in transportation and warehousing as well as wholesale trade 
in September 
Profitability growth by sector and business size in September (YoY%, six-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The cost of maintaining supply chains is rising 
In fact, the prices paid component of the September services reading from the Institute for Supply Management indicates still-
building price pressure for firms (Exhibit 4). This marked the highest reading since July 2022.   
Using Bank of America internal data, we identified payments to logistics and freight companies to assess a key part of supply 
chain costs. In June, logistics and freight payments’ growth per client from small businesses grew nearly 30%, though has 
moderated to 12.7% YoY in September (Exhibit 5). Meanwhile, mid-sized firms’ payments growth in this category was up just 
3.2% YoY in September. 
The divergence suggests logistics costs may be disproportionately affecting smaller firms, who are typically less capable of 
absorbing rising costs than larger firms. Larger companies also often benefit from dedicated logistics contracts and greater 
purchasing power, while smaller ones may be more exposed to spot-market transportation costs and fluctuations in freight 
pricing.

Evidence of more moving parts in companies’ supply chains 
Yet there is increasing evidence that tariffs and geopolitics are adding complexity to small and mid-sized companies’ supply 
chains. 
In Bank of America payments data, average distribution and inventory transaction growth per business client has increased 
steadily since the spring of 2025 despite spending growth in this category falling in September (Exhibit 6). Rising transaction 
growth alongside softer spending growth suggests businesses may be engaging with more suppliers, shipments or inventory 
movements even as overall procurement outlays moderates.  
Furthermore, in May 2026, the Global Supply Chain Pressure Index reached its highest reading since the summer of 2022 and has 
continued to remain elevated into October (Exhibit 7). This further emphasizes that transportation bottlenecks, delays and 
procurement challenges are becoming more common globally. While current readings remain below the extreme levels observed 
during the pandemic, the recent move higher indicates supply chain normalization may be reversing. 
Exhibit 6: Average distribution and inventory transaction growth 
per business client has increased steadily since the spring of 2025

The trials of trucking  
As of October 1, BofA Global Research's proprietary bi-weekly Truckload Demand Indicator (see Methodology) remained below 
its historical growth signal (Exhibit 9) and the Truck Capacity Indicator, which reflects shippers' views of available capacity, 
tightened significantly, falling from 39.2 to 28.4 (Exhibit 8). This suggests shippers are seeing fewer trucks available relative to 
demand than in the previous survey. 
According to BofA Global Research, the current trucking upcycle is being driven by constrained supply rather than a significant 
improvement in freight demand. While any positive movement in demand is an important indicator as the industry moves into 
peak shipping season between late September and early December, tighter capacity appears to be the dominant force 
supporting freight rates. 
Exhibit 8: Shippers’ view of available capacity has fallen 
significantly as of October 1, 2026 
BofA Truckload Capacity Indicator by year (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 9: The Demand Indicator has been sub-60 for the past two 
months after 4.5 months above 
BofA Truckload Demand Indicator (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The reduction in capacity reflects several factors, including stricter commercial driver's license (CDL) and training requirements, 
increased compliance and enforcement measures, and other regulatory changes that have reduced the pool of qualified drivers. 
This has also likely driven some wage inflation within the industry. According to Bank of America payments data, transportation 
and warehousing payroll growth per client has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses (Exhibit 10).  
Having to pay higher wages suggests labor shortages remain a challenge in the sector. Firms may increasingly need to offer 
higher compensation to attract and retain workers as trucking capacity tightens. In turn, such capacity puts upward pressure on 
freight rates, particularly in the spot market where small businesses may be more impacted. 
Exhibit 10: Transportation and warehousing payroll growth has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses 
Payroll payments per client by industry and business size (monthly, YoY%, three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Logistics payments tend to increase in the second half of the year 
September marks a key run-up to the holiday season (read more on signs of holiday spending in the October Consumer 
Checkpoint), with spending on ocean shipping, containers, and other transportation services steadily accelerating ahead of the 
peak shopping period.  
In fact, over the past two years, the month of November has seen ocean shipping/container spending volumes well above their 
2023-2025 monthly average (Exhibit 11). The seasonal pattern likely reflects inventory restocking and holiday-related shipping 
activity. Ocean shipping volumes typically accelerate during the fall months as businesses prepare for year-end demand. 
Exhibit 11: Ocean shipping/container spending volumes were well above their 2023-2025 monthly average in November 
Logistics and freight average payments by category (monthly, indexed, 100 = monthly 2023-2025 average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Distributors and inventory payments’ growth fell ahead of holiday season 
Changes in logistics and freight spending often provide an early signal of inventory trends, as distributors account for a large 
share of supply chain-related spending (Exhibit 12). 
The sharp moderation in distribution and inventory spending growth per client for both small and mid-sized businesses indicates 
that much of the large-scale inventory accumulation seen earlier in 2025 has already occurred (Exhibit 13). In other words, 
businesses appear to be replenishing inventories for seasonal demand rather than significantly expanding stockpiles as they did 
earlier in the year. 
Overall, businesses seem to be entering the holiday season in relatively stable financial condition, supported by tariff refunds 
and positive profitability growth. However, rising logistics costs, tighter trucking capacity and elevated supply chain pressures 
suggest operating conditions are becoming more challenging.

chain-related payments

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business and/or Business Banking clients with a deposit account or a 
Small Business credit card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of 
America per Business client data represents activity spending from active Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card and at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and are 
generally defined as under $5mm in annual sales revenue. Mid-sized businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America 
and are generally defined as $5mm-$50mm in annual sales revenue. 
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Revenue tiers are determined by the combination of following factors: 1) stated revenue on small businesses credit applications, 2) actual 
account inflow into Bank of America Deposit Accounts, and 3) third party revenue estimation.  
The alternative hiring indicator consists of payments from Bank of America business clients to business-focused hiring firms which include both 
direct deposits through Automated Clearing House (ACH) and payments via credit and debit cards. 
Supply chain-related spending is comprised of payments to logistics, freight, distribution, inventory, delivery service, and trucking companies. 
These are identified from merchant names and MCC codes. 
BofA Global Research’s trucking indicators are a bi-weekly diffusion-style survey of shipper demand expectations over the next 0–3 months, 
built from responses from surveyed shippers. 
Data regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Existing Home Sales • Thursday
Housing market conditions remain highly unfavorable. Mortgage
rates are the primary headwind, and they have climbed even higher
alongside the rise in longer-term yields. At the same time, low
supply is preventing a broad correction in home prices, leaving
prospective buyers squeezed on two fronts.
We expect that existing home sales slid 3.4% in August to an
annualized rate of 3.92 million. Behind this forecast is ongoing

weakness in mortgage applications and a recent bounce in
mortgage rates, which rose from 6.43% at the beginning of July to
6.66% by month-end.
We do not expect affordability conditions to meaningfully improve
in the near term. Supply is likely to remain constrained while high
mortgage rates discourage mobility. And mortgage rates are apt
to remain elevated amid firm economic growth expectations and
heavy government and corporate debt issuance.
(Return)

### Page 3

Economics Week Ahead Economics
CPI • Friday
Headline inflation looks to have picked up last month. We estimate
the CPI rose 0.40% in August, with gasoline prices increasing a little
over 4% as renewed tensions in the Middle East pushed oil higher.
Grocery prices also likely rebounded after modestly declining in July.
Outside of food and energy, price pressures appear little changed.
We expect the core CPI to rise 0.23%, essentially matching July's

pace. Core goods inflation should remain firm, led by another solid
increase in used vehicle prices as consumer prices continue to
catch up to the trend indicated at wholesale auctions. Meantime,
household furnishings and other tariff-sensitive categories appear
to be stabilizing after declining earlier this year.
Core services inflation should be little changed with another 0.2%
monthly increase. But underneath the surface, we expect a modest
pickup in travel (led by lodging away from home) to offset slightly

cooler medical care and housing cost growth.
All told, we expect the report to show that while headline inflation
remains influenced by the ebbs and flows of the conflict in the
Middle East, core inflation remains contained. Should our forecast
prove correct, core CPI will ease to 2.4% year-over-year, in line with
its 6-month annualized rate but above the more recent 3-month
annualized pace of 1.7%.

### Page 4

Weekly Economics
G10 Week Ahead
European Central Bank Monetary Policy Meeting •
Thursday
We expect the European Central Bank (ECB) to raise the Deposit
Rate by 25 bps to 2.50% next week. August headline inflation
remained elevated at 3.3% year-over-year, though softer core
and services inflation suggest underlying pressures continue to
moderate. Still, the more hawkish tone of the July minutes and
renewed rise in energy prices should keep policymakers cautious.

The recent escalation in the Middle East has pushed fuel prices
at the pump close to their 2022 peaks, while natural gas storage
remains below historical levels, leaving the Eurozone vulnerable
to further energy price increases. The ECB is unlikely to respond
aggressively if the shock remains confined to energy given the
downside risks to growth. However, a broader rise in food inflation
linked to El Niño would be more concerning, as highly visible prices

could lift inflation expectations and generate second-round effects.
For now, resilient Q3 activity, elevated headline inflation and
contained wage growth support one final hike. The ECB will likely
retain and emphasize its scenario-based outlook in the updated
staff projections given continued uncertainty around the conflict
and energy prices. The policy path beyond September will depend
on whether energy and food pressures persist and begin to spread,

which could leave room for further tightening.
(Return)

U.K. Monthly GDP • Friday
We expect UK GDP to rise 0.2% month-over-month in July, with
growth of 0.3% on a three-month-over-three-month basis.

Activity likely moderated after June’s strong rebound, though
services remained supportive as the July services PMI rose to 52.1.
Retail sales volumes fell 0.5% month-over-month, while softer
manufacturing activity likely reflected some reversal of earlier
precautionary stockbuilding.
The moderation appears more consistent with payback from
June than a broader deterioration in momentum. August PMIs
remain consistent with Q3 GDP growth of around 0.3% quarter-

over-quarter. Still, the renewed escalation in the Middle East and
associated rise in gas and energy prices adds upside risk to inflation
and could weigh on household demand. Against this backdrop and
with Bank of England hawks continuing to emphasize the need for
tighter policy, we expect the BoE to remain cautious and continue
to look for a rate hike in Q4. -1.0%

Mexico CPI • Wednesday
We expect Mexico’s August inflation rate to edge higher, with
headline CPI rising to 3.34% year-over-year (0.27% month-over-
month) from 3.26% year-over-year in early August. Part of the
pickup likely reflects less favorable base effects and a smaller
disinflationary contribution from food prices. While agricultural
prices continued to help restrain inflation in mid-August, the drag

was notably less pronounced than in July. Core inflation should
remain elevated at 3.98% year-over-year (0.25% month-over-
month).
While inflation remains within Banxico’s midpoint 3% target,
persistent core and services inflation suggest convergence toward
the midpoint of the target could prove gradual. For now, we
continue to expect Banxico to keep rates on hold, with the policy
rate remaining at 6.50% through the rest of 2026 and into early
2027 as policymakers await clearer evidence that underlying

inflation pressures are easing sustainably. While rate cuts remain
a possibility in 2027, the current inflation backdrop argues for
patience in the near term.

Brazil CPI • Friday
We expect Brazil’s August inflation rate to slow to 4.20% year-over-
year, helped by lower electricity, food and fuel prices. The main
driver should be a temporary reduction in residential electricity bills
stemming from a one-off credit, while recent data already point to
a notable easing in price pressures. Mid-August CPI came in weaker
than expected, with inflation falling to 4.24% year-over-year amid
declines in electricity, food, fuel and airfare prices. Beyond these

temporary factors, restrictive monetary policy continues to weigh
on economic activity, as evidenced by the recent slowdown in Q2
GDP growth. However, underlying inflation risks remain. A still-
tight labor market and government measures aimed at supporting
consumption could keep services inflation sticky, making progress
toward the 3.0% inflation target more gradual.
Overall, the inflation outlook remains consistent with additional
policy easing. We expect COPOM to deliver a 25 bps rate cut in

September, bringing the Selic Rate to 13.75%. Beyond September,
policymakers are likely to proceed cautiously as they assess
incoming inflation data and the impact of the election cycle on
inflation expectations. Our base case remains for a pause after
September, with additional easing more likely in 2027.

Document 367: France’s manufacturing rebound offers some relief, but no turning point yet

French manufacturing output rose for a second consecutive
month in September, led by a sharp rebound in car production.
But with output still below last year’s level and construction
ﬁrmly in recession, industry can only soften, not reverse, the
economy’s stagnation
Charlotte de Montpellier
French manufacturing output rose for a second consecutive month in
September
Manufacturing output returns to its June
level
There is ﬁnally some better news from the French economy.

Manufacturing output increased by 0.5% in September, following a
0.3% rise in August, bringing production back to its June level. The
improvement was relatively broad-based. Food production
rebounded by 1.0%, while output of electrical, electronic and

computer equipment rose by 2.6%, reaching its highest level since
November 2023. Transport equipment production also increased by
2.7%, largely thanks to a 7.5% rebound in the automotive sector.
However, September’s ﬁgures look more like a welcome recovery
from previous weakness than the start of a sustained industrial
upturn. Manufacturing output was still 0.9% below its level a year
earlier. Transport equipment remained one of the main areas of

weakness, with production down 3.8% year-on-year. Despite
September’s strong monthly increase, automotive production was
still 9.3% lower than a year ago.
The broader industrial picture was also less encouraging than the
manufacturing data alone suggest. Total industrial production fell
by 0.5% in September because of lower electricity output and
remained 0.6% below its level a year earlier.
Construction remains deeply depressed
Construction did not see a similar improvement. Output declined

by a further 0.2% in September and was 4.5% lower than a year
earlier. The downturn continues to aﬀect every major segment of
the sector. Civil engineering output dropped by 6.6% year-on-year,
while building construction fell by 4.8% and specialised
construction activities by 4.2%.
These ﬁgures conﬁrm that construction remains a big drag on the
French economy. Elevated ﬁnancing costs, weak demand and the
postponement of investment projects are likely to keep the sector

under pressure in the coming months.
Industry should outperform the rest of the
economy
October’s business surveys were relatively encouraging for
industry, particularly compared with the deterioration in other
sectors. Combined with a favourable statistical carry-over from the
third quarter, this suggests that manufacturing could support
activity in the ﬁnal months of the year.

Still, the surveys predate the social unrest of 17 October, while order
books remain weak, especially for exports, and tighter ﬁnancing
conditions are weighing on investment and inventories. Industry
should therefore outperform services and construction in the fourth
quarter, but not by enough to generate a broader recovery. After
GDP contracted by 0.1% in the third quarter, growth is likely to
remain close to zero.
Domestic demand is unlikely to provide

much support
The outlook outside industry is weak. The rebound in household
consumption in the third quarter is unlikely to last, given the sharp
fall in consumer conﬁdence, growing concerns about
unemployment and renewed pressure on real incomes. Investment
is also likely to remain subdued. Weak demand, elevated borrowing
costs and uncertainty surrounding taxes and public spending are
encouraging both companies and households to delay projects.
Credit conditions have tightened particularly sharply in France, with

a net 27% of banks reporting stricter lending standards for
businesses in the third quarter.
As a result, the modest support provided by manufacturing will
probably be outweighed by continued weakness in consumption,
investment and construction. We expect French GDP growth of only
0.3% in 2026 and 0.5% in 2027, with risks still tilted to the
downside.
Weak growth leaves even less room for
ﬁscal manoeuvre
The budget process has since reached a new impasse. After 11

days of debate, the National Assembly unanimously rejected the
revenue side of the 2027 budget on Wednesday evening, by 458
votes. The amendments adopted during the debate would have
widened the deﬁcit to an estimated 5.9% of GDP, well above the
government’s 5% target.
The government’s initial draft will now go to the right-leaning
Senate, which is expected to rewrite the budget by shifting more of

Document 434: Swing State_ Navigating Midterm Volatility (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Swing State_ Navigating Midterm Volatility (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 14
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Swing State: Navigating 
Midterm Volatility

1 
Authors 
Laura Daniels 
Deputy Associate Director of Equity  
Research

Josh Ray 
Associate Director of Equity 
Research

Matthew Barnard 
Head of US Company Research

Parag Thatte 
Strategist

Brett Ryan 
Senior US Economist

October 23, 2026 
A client-facing version of this report is available at the Deutsche Bank Research 
Portal. The following Deutsche Bank Research analysts contributed to this report: 
Brett Ryan (econ), Binky Chadha and Parag Thatte (equity strategy), Benjamin 
Soff (data centers), Daniel Knauff (software), George Hill (healthcare), Omotayo 
Okusanya (REITs), Peter Abramowitz (office REITs), Mark DeVries (consumer and 
specialty finance), Nicole DeBlase (MI/EE), Corinne Blanchard (clean tech), 
Richa Harnain (transports), and Scott Deuschle (aerospace and defense).  
With the 2026 US midterm elections approaching, we look at key investor 
debates surrounding the event and legislative priorities of the next Congress. 
Despite the uncertainty that midterms can bring, the market appears fairly 
confident on expectations for the overall results. While a surprise Republican-
majority victory in both chambers is still possible, consensus is converging 
around current polling indicating Democrats are likely to flip the seats needed for 
a House majority and may win a Senate majority. Historically, an incumbent 
president has lost seats in 20 of the last 22 midterms. Investor expectations are 
in line with this. Polymarket, for example, is currently above 90% for Democrats 
gaining control of the House and above 60% for Democrats gaining control of the 
Senate.  
With this set of expectations, key investor debates instead focus on the 
legislative agenda for hot-ticket policy issues. Namely, these hinge on AI and 
data centers, affordability (affecting healthcare, housing, and education loans), 
and economic and fiscal policy. Debates also surround possible influence of the 
new Congress on the war in Iran and, by extension, energy prices. In this report, 
we cut through the noise on what the next Congress could reasonably advance 
from these agendas, what the impact would be on affected sectors, and how to 
address market uncertainty – and perceived certainty – into the elections. 
Historically, midterm elections have proven to be a check on administration 
policies. The number of seats lost in the midterms by the incumbent party is tied 
to the president’s approval rating going into midterms, which in turn tends to 
align with consumer confidence. President Trump’s ~38% approval rating, at a 
record low for his tenure, would suggest a little over a 45-seat loss for the 
Republican Party (in the House and Senate combined). That said, polling in the 
Senate races that will determine control of the chamber largely remains within 
the margin of error, and in 2024, Trump won in at least five of the seven core toss-
up states. Thus, the Senate remains a much closer call than the House race.

### Page 2

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: Presidential Approval vs. House Seats

Source: Real Clear Politics

Figure 2: Presidential Approval vs. Consumer Confidence

Source: The Conference Board

Whatever the outcome of the  midterms, the legislative agenda will 
still face constraints from the White House. It is all but impossible for either party 
to gain a veto -proof majority  in either chamber, minimizing the likelihood of a 
large shift in fiscal or regulatory policy. The most  immediately obvious impact of 
Democrats taking the Senate would be the ability to slow or block executive 
branch appointees, including at the Federal Reserve Board of Governors or the 
Supreme Court, should any members step down before their terms end.

### Page 3

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Without garnering a two-thirds majority in both chambers, Democrats lack a clear 
path to unwind the 2025 tax cuts or enact AI-related regulatory legislation – at 
least, not without President Trump’s explicit approval. With respect to the near -
term fiscal policy outlook, the most important period for investors may be the 
“lame duck” session, the period after the November 3 election and before the new 
Congress convenes on January 3. Recall that current federal government funding 
expires on December 11, and the Trump administration is asking for a nearly 60% 
increase in defense spending, the largest since the Korean War.  In addition, the 
debt limit will become binding again around the middle of next year. In short, the 
actions during the lame duck session could determine whether the new Congress 
is seated amid a government shutdown, a contentious battle over the federal 
budget, and the debt limit looming in the background.  
In terms of the market reaction to midterm  elections, the risk-reward profile has 
typically been favorable , with 21 of the last 23 midterm years  seeing positive 
returns in the fourth quarter, with an average gain of 7% in the S&P. The gains are 
notable even after adjusting for typical positive year-end seasonality.

Figure 3: S&P Returns and Midterms

Source: Bloomberg Finance LP

It w ould not be unusual for the market to go sideways as investors hedge 
potential volatility and uncertainty, as we have seen previously ahead of other 
well-telegraphed pivotal events. This has historically been followed by a rally as 
the results became clear and  uncertainty dissipated, regardless of the result 
itself. In the current context, if election polls and prediction market probabilities 
continue being lopsided, the market may not treat the election as an uncertain 
risk event going in. Uncertainty could still ramp up after, and the typical midterm 
election rally may be pushed out. It is notable that the one prominent exception 
to the midterm rallies was in 2018 , when the market sold off sharply after the 
elections as uncertainty spiked around trade war 1.0 as well as Fed policy.

### Page 4

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

We note that the midterm elections also coincide with the third quarter earnings 
season, during which we expect companies to deliver growth of 34%. For next 
year, we expect growth to be a still -robust 17% with risks to the upside. 
Eventually, earnings tend to be the anchor for equities and have been for several 
years despite numerous domestic political as well as geopolitical shocks. 
Across sectors, most have rallied around past midterms in keeping with the 
market, but there isn’t a consistent pattern of out - or underperformance . This 
suggests that for the initial conditions for each sector, specific policies and 
positioning played a bigger role.  For that reason, we turn to a closer analysis of 
key legislative and policy debates impacting exposed sectors.

### Page 5

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

AI and Data Centers  
AI and data centers have become a campaign talking point for candidates from 
both the Democratic and Republican parties. This has spurred investor questions 
on whether to expect a national regulatory framework constraining data center 
buildouts. Some of th e headlines surrounding this debate overestimate what 
Congress can  accomplish through national legislation compared with what is 
possible at a state level, particularly during a presidential administration 
supportive of AI and data center acceleration. Still, the industry is responding to 
a political backdrop that is increasingly scrutinizing AI and data centers.  
The current administration has prioritized advancing AI and digital infrastructure 
development. The White House ’s AI Action Plan , released July 2025, included a 
set of executive orders supporting the rapid buildout of AI data centers by 
reducing certain federal regulatory and permitting burdens. The administration 
did not preempt state laws on data center infrastructure development and 
federal permitting orders generally do not override state zoning and utility rules. 
In March 2026 , the White House announced the Ratepayer Protection Pledge, 
under which large data center operators would voluntarily commit to covering 
the cost to upgrade generation, transmission, substations, or other necessary 
infrastructure to service these developments.  The industry’s response to these 
proposals has been largely constructive. The Data Center Coalition  (DCC), an 
industry group representing major  hyperscalers and data center  owners and 
operators came out in support of the initial version of the Pledge, while arguing 
that large -load customers across all industries also should cover the costs to 
upgrade energy infrastructure. According to the DCC , “the data center 
industry remains committed to being responsible partners where we  operate, 
which includes  paying our full energy costs  and investing in local jobs and 
workforce development.” 
Congress voted in September 2026 to codify the principles present in the Pledge 
via the Ratepayer Protection Act. The bill, which only required states to “consider” 
these standards, passed overwhelmingly in the House (417-3), but failed to reach 
60 votes in the Senate (57 -43). Critics noted the bill stopped short of imposing 
mandatory nationwide rules. 
Looking forward, we expect a Congress that is more focused on consumer 
protection might look to codify a more stringent version of these rules, requiring 
large-load customers to bear the full incremental costs associated with 
generation, transmission, and distribution upgrades.  However, even if such a 
framework were passed into law, we believe the overall economic impact to large 
data center operators would be relatively modest, for two reasons. 
For one, the White House would maintain a veto over  bills that include language 
it views as overly burdensome towards AI infrastructure development . Two, data 
center operators  generally underwrite projects only when they have a high 
degree of confidence in securing power and, where necessary, may already be 
embedding the cost of upgrading energy infrastructure into their investment 
plans. We expect developers to be capable of passing on sustained increases in 
power infrastructure costs to their customers , which, for many 100  MW+ 
developments, are the major investment-grade hyperscalers.

### Page 6

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 6

Though data center development is receiving increasing national attention, it is 
still largely a local issue, and the most consequential elections will be at the state 
and local levels. We have seen a number of state -driven actions on data center s 
in recent months, including: 
• In July, New York State’s governor issued an executive order to establish 
a temporary moratorium on data center development  while the state 
develops a standardized framework of rules for key issues (including 
responsibilities for power and energy infrastructure upgrades; additional 
power generation; clean energy; water use, emission, and waste 
management; and state/local financ ial incentives). The New York 
legislature also passed a one -year moratorium on Department of 
Environmental Conservation permits and approvals for 20  MW+ data 
center developments. 
• In August, Texas’ governor directed the state’s public utility commission 
to conduct a comprehensive audit of every data center advancing 
through the ERCOT (Texas’s grid operator) interconnection process. The 
audit will cover several notable areas including public financial support, 
electricity demand and supply, water use, community impacts, and 
ownership and control. 
• In September, California’s governor passed a comprehensive regulatory 
package, covering electricity, water, reporting, and environmental 
review. Most of these provisions take effect January 1, 2027, and phase 
in through 2027 -2028, but the package does not ban data centers or 
impose a statewide moratorium on development activity. 
• Oregon, Pennsylvania, New Jersey, and other states have also 
established orders to create standards and frameworks governing data 
center development (but without imposing broad moratoriums). 
At the more local level, a large number of moratoriums have been passed on new 
data center development. Data collected by SemiAnalysis, an independent 
research firm, shows that, as of September, more than 300 local data  center 
moratoriums have been adopted nationwide across counties, municipalities, 
townships, and other local governments.  
However, these state and local moratoriums appear  thus far to be less impactful 
to near- and medium-term development activity than headline coverage might 
otherwise suggest. According to SemiAnalysis, only about 2.3 GW of the 38 GW 
of data center capacity planned or under construction for 2027 has faced delays 
as a result of  the New York state executive order (0.8 GW) and/or local 
moratoriums (1.5 GW). 
We believe the primary reasons that moratoriums are, in reality, less likely to 
impact near -term development activity are (1) that these moratoriums largely 
impact new projects and applications (rather than existing developments in  
progress), (2) there may be flexibility to relocate or rezone early-stage projects in 
response to moratoriums, and (3) data center developers are increasingly 
partnering with local communities to gain their support via investments and other 
programs (as we wrote about recently).  Potential regulatory risk has not yet 
slowed capex spending patterns, with capex spend still expected to climb from

### Page 7

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 7

$0.5 trillion in 2025 to $1.3 trillion by 2027; data centers typically represent ~20-
30% of the total bill of materials for new AI capacity.  
Although the practical impact on near- and medium-term development pipelines 
appears less pronounced than headlines might suggest, all this is not to say that 
data centers will not face increasing local opposition following the midterms. 
Indeed, i n our conversations with industry participants, there is an 
acknowledgment that greater public scrutiny of data centers is not going away.  
Additional moratoriums, zoning restrictions, permitting requirements, or utility -
related conditions could affect the pace, cost , and overall feasibility of future 
projects.

### Page 8

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 8

Affordability Agenda: Healthcare, Housing, and 
Education Loans  
The affordability agenda has become a centerpiece of many Democratic as well 
as Republican campaign platforms.  This platform is impacting investor 
debates across healthcare, housing, and student loans. In healthcare,  the push 
among some Democrats for expanded health coverage under the Medicare for All 
banner is placing pressure on managed care stocks, but meanwhile, companies 
with exposure to Medicaid and  the Affordable Care Act (ACA) could see a 
supportive political backdrop. On housing, investor debates on the impacts of the 
affordability agenda have continued since passage of legislation in July 2026 that 
caps the number of single -family homes for -profit institutional investors can 
purchase. Affordability of higher education is likewise part of the conversatio n, 
leading investors to debate which levers Congress could pull that would affect 
student loan companies.  
Healthcare 
In healthcare, w e see the upcoming midterm elections as a sentiment event for 
managed care stocks , not a policy event.  With the presidential veto in place 
through 2028, we do not expect the government to enact direct healthcare 
legislation during the next Congress. Forecasts place the probability of a change 
in House control at roughly 79%, with both chambers changing in about 44% of 
simulations. Although investor skittishness around Medicare for All (M4A) rhetoric 
is high, we see the likelihood of M4A replacing private coverage as a near -zero 
probability in this timeframe.  
M4A has no realistic legislative path before 2029, but the rhetoric weighs on 
managed care organization (MCO) stock multiples by raising perceived long-term 
risk rather than affecting near -term earnings. Polling from  Kaiser Family 
Foundation, a health policy research organization, shows support for M4A at a 
decade-plus high, and the 2018 –2019 cycle suggests that increased campaign 
attention can pressure MCO multiples even without a direct policy impact. We 
expect similar sentiment -driven pressure through year -end and potentially into 
the 2028 primary cycle but would not view it as a change i n near -term 
fundamentals.  
For Medicare Advantage, we see a h eadline risk over earnings reality. The near-
term earnings impact for affected companies should be limited, because the US 
Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS) has finalized a 2.48% Medicare 
Advantage (MA) rate increase for 2027, versus 0.09% in the advance notice. A 
change in House control would instead increase focus on 2028 payment policy, 
risk adj ustment, and prior authorization. Coding reform already has bipartisan 
support: sponsors estimate that the Cassidy -Merkley No UPCODE Act could 
generate up to $275  billion of savings over ten years, while MedPAC estimates 
coding intensity will add roughly $40  billion to 2025 Medicare Advantage  costs 
relative to traditional Medicare. In our view, the more immediate consideration is 
potential multiple pressure rather than a measurable change to company 
earnings.  
However, the next Congress could b enefit Medicaid funding and ACA policy. 
Medicaid and the ACA appear relatively better positioned, although any benefit

### Page 9

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 9

would likely be reflected first in sentiment and lower perceived policy risk rather 
than near -term estimate revisions. Medicaid community engagement 
requirements take effect in most states by January 1, 2027. Although repeal 
remains unlikely given the presidential veto, a change in House control could shift 
the year -end funding debate toward  restoring or  preserving federal Medicaid 
support, which could ease state budget pressure and support rate -setting. The 
ACA policy backdrop could also become more supportive, although we see 
limited scope for enhanced premium subsidies to return before 2029 after the 
three-year extension passed by the House stalled.  
Housing 
Turning to the housing sector, the likely midterm outcome of a split government 
would result in more gridlock. Historically, political gridlock in terms of passing 
new regulation has been a tailwind for the real estate sector, reducing regulatory 
risk at the federal level. In the scenario of a split Congress, in which the Democrats 
have a majority in the House but not the Senate, bills with bipartisan support have 
a greater likelihood to become law. Two key areas in which this bipartisan support 
exists are housing affordability and consumer protection. 
Among real estate investment trusts ( REITs), we see Residential as among the 
most impacted subsectors, particularly as it relates to the affordability debate. 
Regardless of the election outcome, housing affordability has become front and 
center on the political agenda  across both parties. F ederal legislation  is more 
likely to target supply -side solutions, as legislation restricting rent increases is 
typically handled at the local and state level. We think Multifamily would be the 
most likely subsector impacted, as a push to expand rental housing supply could 
lead to eased zoning restrictions, particularly in supply -constrained coastal 
markets that have historically benefited from higher barriers to entry. That said, 
this would likely take years to materialize. With a Democratic majority , Congress 
could voice opposition to the influence of large institutional landlords, but we see 
this strictly as a sentiment risk to stocks.  
Single Family For Rent (SFR) is less exposed to near -term legislative shocks, as 
the sector was a focus of the 21st Century ROAD to Housing Act passed in July 
2026 over the summer. The  measure impacts institutional owners’ ability to buy 
homes off the MLS but contains specific carve-out provisions that will allow them 
to keep developing build -to-rent (BTR) product and buy excess inventory from 
homebuilders, the sector's preferred acquis ition channel in recent years. The 
ROAD to Housing Act also contained provisions that could theoretically ease the 
production of manufactured homes, the main business segment for 
Manufactured Housing (MH) REITs. The first-order impact is likely more flexibility 
in home designs that could lead to further densification of MH communities, 
although we note emerging investor debate about the long -term impacts of 
increased MH production on competitive supply for a property type t hat has 
historically been supply constrained, mainly due to NIMBY-ism.  
Parallel to the conversation on affordability, it is clear that, regardless of which 
political party may be in power following the midterms, a major focus remains 
addressing the US fiscal deficit and its continued growth. We continue to pay 
attention to potential budget cuts or sequestration to slow government spending 
and reduce the deficit, which could have an impact on real estate.  Key areas to 
watch include cuts to healthcare spending (which would be a negative for skilled

### Page 10

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 10

nursing focused REITs, given Medicare is a major revenue source for their tenant 
base) and cuts that would impact Office REITs with large government exposure.  
We also worry that an elevated federal deficit and the elevated Treasury 
borrowing needs today create a risk that interest rates will remain  higher for 
longer. This puts pressure on real estate valuations and cost of financing for REITs 
to drive external growth via acquisitions and development. A split Congress is 
likely to result in more difficult budget negotiations and increased oversight, but 
it remains unclear w hat the ultimate outcome would be to address the budget 
deficit issue. 
With real estate being more dependent on local market dynamics, we believe the 
more likely regulatory impact would come from local regulation. This could show 
up on the ballots during the midterms , with increased possibility of rent control 
legislation targeted at residential real estate as well as self -storage, as cities and 
municipalities focus on affordability issues. There is also growing legislative 
pushback at the local level against development of real assets such as data 
centers given the large power requirements, as discussed in detail elsewhere in 
this report.  
Education Lending 
Midterm campaign platforms on education affordability  have a bearing on the 
consumer and specialty finance sector. Much of the policy exposure in this sector 
is through the executive branch bodies, not Congress, however. This includes, for 
example, regulatory authorities  such as the FDIC (Federal Deposit Insurance 
Corporation), the CFPB (Consumer Financial Protection Bureau ), or the FHFA 
(Federal Housing Finance Agency, which regulates Fannie Mae and Freddie 
Mac). That said, historical data shows that student loan company stocks can still 
directionally align with midterm party outcome and expectations  in Congress, 
although the sample size is small.
